are mean zero, sub-Gaussian, and weakly correlated in the sense that:
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{1}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{i}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{2}}
, where
\boldsymbol{Z}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}\times k_{2}}
has independent mean zero sub-Gaussian entries, and for
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{1}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}\times k_{1}}
,
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}\times k_{2}}
, assume that
0<\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{j}}\|_{2}<c_{0}
for
j=1,2
.

(b)

The elements of
\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}
are mean zero, sub-Gaussian, and weakly correlated in the same sense as above. Furthermore, assume that

\displaystyle\text{for }i,k\in[p_{1}],i\neq k,\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{p_{2}}u_{ij}u_{kj}\right]\leq c_{1}\sqrt{\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}};

\displaystyle\text{and for }i,k\in[p_{2}],i\neq k,\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{p_{1}}u_{ji}u_{jk}\right]\leq c_{1}\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}}.

Assumption 20
.

There exist universal constants
c_{1},c_{2}
, and
C_{2}
such that

(a)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{j}\|_{F}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}\Tr\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{U}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{\top}\mid\boldsymbol{F}\right]\right)<c_{1}.

(b)

c_{2}<\lambda_{\min}(\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{F}/n)<\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{F}/n)<C_{2}
, and
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{f}_{i}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{f}_{i}^{\top}\xrightarrow{P}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}
for some
k_{1}\!k_{2}\times k_{1}\!k_{2}
positive definite matrix
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}
, where
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{f}}_{i}:=\vect(\boldsymbol{F}_{i})
and
\mathbb{F}=\big(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{f}}_{1}\cdots\widetilde{\boldsymbol{f}}_{n}\big)^{\top}
.

Assumption 21
.

There exist universal constants
c_{1},c_{2}
,
C_{1}
, and
C_{2}
such that

(a)

\nu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{H}_{i})\leq C_{i}\nu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{H}_{i})
, for
i=1,2
.

(b)

\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{W}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}/p_{i})\geq c_{i}
, for
i=1,2
.

Assumption 22
.

(a)

There exists a constant
M<\infty
such that the
k_{1}\!k_{2}\times k_{1}\!k_{2}
matrix satisfies

\mathbb{E}\left\|\frac{1}{np_{1}\!p_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{1}\!p_{2}}[\boldsymbol{W}_{2}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}]_{j}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{f}}_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{u}_{ij}\right\|^{2}\leq M,

where
[\boldsymbol{W}_{2}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}]_{j}
is the
j
-th column of
(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top})
,
\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{f}_{i}^{\top}
is the
i
-th row of
\mathbb{F}
, and
\widetilde{u}_{ij}
is the
(i,j)
-th element of
\mathbb{U}
.

(b)

There exists a full-rank
k_{1}\!k_{2}\times k_{1}k_{2}
matrix
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}
such that

\plim_{p_{1},p_{2}\rightarrow\infty}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}.

This also implies
\nu_{\min}(\boldsymbol{H}_{i}),\nu_{\max}(\boldsymbol{H}_{i})=\Omega_{p}(1)
, for
i=1,2
.

(c)

There exists an
k_{1}\!k_{2}\times k_{1}\!k_{2}
matrix
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{U}
such that

\lim_{p_{1},p_{2}\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{p_{1}\!p_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{1}\!p_{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{p_{1}\!p_{2}}[\boldsymbol{W}_{2}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}]_{j}[\boldsymbol{W}_{2}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}]_{k}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{u}_{ij}\widetilde{u}_{ik}]=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{U},\text{ for }i\in[n].

(d)

Assume that there exist a
k_{1}\!k_{2}\times k_{1}\!k_{2}
matrix
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
and a
p_{1}\!p_{2}\times p_{1}\!p_{2}
matrix
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
such that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{f}}_{i}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}^{\top}\xrightarrow{d}{\cal M}{\cal N}(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{\Phi},\boldsymbol{\Psi}),

where
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}=\vect(\boldsymbol{U}_{i})
,
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
is divided into
p_{2}\times p_{2}
blocks, denoted as
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{1}}
, for
i,j\in[p_{2}]
, and that
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{ij}={\rm o_{p}}\left(p^{-1}\right)
for all
i\neq j
.

(e)

There exists
M\leq\infty
such that for all
n
,
p_{1},
and
p_{2}
,
\mathbb{E}\widetilde{u}_{ij}=0
,
\mathbb{E}|\widetilde{u}_{ij}|^{8}\leq M
.

Appendix D
Theoretical Analysis of “Pre-trained Projection” and “Block-wise Averaging”

D.1
Proof of Proposition
7

We denote
\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{1}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}\boldsymbol{R}^{*}{\boldsymbol{R}^{*}}^{\top}
,
\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{2}=\frac{1}{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{1}=\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{X}_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{i}^{\top}}
, and
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{S}_{2}=\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{X}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{i}}
.
We first present a technical lemma that bounds the
\ell_{2}
norm between
\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{1}
(resp.
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{S}_{2}
) and
\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{1}
(resp.
\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{2}
). Its proof is provided at the end of this section.

Lemma 23
.

Under Assumption
5
and
19
, there exists a universal constant
c
such that

\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{1}\right\|_{2}\leq c\left(\sqrt{t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})}\sqrt{\frac{\log k_{1}+t}{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{1}}}\right),

and

\left\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{S}_{2}-\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{2}\right\|_{2}\leq c\left(\sqrt{t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})}\sqrt{\frac{\log k_{2}+t}{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{2}}}\right)

with probability at least
1-11e^{-t}

Proof of Proposition
7
.

Since the proof for
\boldsymbol{H}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{H}_{2}
are the same, we only provide that for
\boldsymbol{H}_{1}
as follows. The argument below is deterministic, conditioned on the event in Lemma
23
.

Let
\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times k_{1}}
be the matrix consists of the top
k_{1}
top eigenvectors of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{1}
. Then
\boldsymbol{W}_{1}=\sqrt{p_{1}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}
. Since
\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{1}
is symmetric, it has the eigen decomposition
\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{1}=\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{*}{\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}}^{\top}
, where
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{*}
is a
k_{1}\times k_{1}
diagonal matrix, and
\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times k_{1}}
satisfies
{\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}=\boldsymbol{I}_{k_{1}}
. Hence,
\boldsymbol{R}^{*}=\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{*}}^{1/2}
.
The upper bound is derived from

\displaystyle\nu_{\max}\left(\frac{1}{p_{1}}\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{k_{1}-1}}\left\|\frac{1}{p_{1}}{\boldsymbol{R}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{1}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{1}}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{*}}^{1/2}\right\|\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{k_{1}-1}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{k_{1}-1}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{1}\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{k_{1}-1}}\left\|\boldsymbol{u}\right\|_{2}=C_{1}

for some universal positive constant
C_{1}
, where the second inequality is because of Assumption
6
.

As for the lower bound, we have

\nu_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{p_{1}}\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\right)\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{1}}}\nu_{\min}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}\right)\nu_{\min}\left({\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{*}}^{1/2}\right)\geq c_{1}\nu_{\min}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}\right),

(35)

where the last inequality follows from Assumption
6
.
Let
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{p_{1}}
be the eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{1}
in the non-decreasing order, and let
\widehat{\lambda}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\lambda}_{p_{1}}
be the eigenvalues of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{1}
in the non-decreasing order. Then by Assumption
6
and that
\rank(\boldsymbol{S})=k_{1}
, we have

\lambda_{k_{1}}\geq c_{1}\text{ for some constant }c_{1},\text{ and }\lambda_{k_{1}+1}=0.

(36)

Let
\delta:=\sqrt{t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})}\sqrt{\frac{\log k_{1}+t}{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{1}}}
.
By Weyl’s Theorem and Lemma
23
, there exists a universal constant
c_{2}
such that

\left|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{i}\right|\leq\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{1}\right\|_{2}\leq c_{2}\delta,\text{ for all }i\in[p_{1}].

(37)

with probability at least
1-11e^{-t}
.

We argue that

\nu_{\min}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}\right)\geq\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{2c_{2}}{c_{1}}\delta\right)^{2}}\geq 1-\frac{2c_{2}}{c_{1}}\delta.

It suffices to prove the above inequality for
\delta<c_{1}/(2c_{2})
; otherwise, the inequality is trivial.
In this case, combining (
36
) and (
37
), we bound the eigen-gap between
\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{1}
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}:=

\displaystyle\inf\left\{|\lambda-\widehat{\lambda}|\mid\lambda\in[\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{1}],\widehat{\lambda}\in(-\infty,\widehat{\lambda}_{k_{1}+1}]\right\}={{\lambda_{k_{1}}-\widehat{\lambda}_{k_{1}+1}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\lambda_{k_{1}}-\lambda_{k_{1}+1}+\lambda_{k_{1}+1}-\widehat{\lambda}_{k_{1}+1}}\geq c_{1}-|\lambda_{k_{1}+1}-\widehat{\lambda}_{k_{1}+1}|\geq c_{1}-c_{2}\delta\geq\frac{c_{1}}{2}.

Davis-Kahan
\sin\Theta
theorem shows that

\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{Z}^{*})\|_{2}\leq\frac{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{1}\|_{2}}{\widetilde{\delta}}\leq\frac{2c_{2}}{c_{1}}\delta.

Together with the fact that

\left[\sin\Theta(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{Z}^{*})\right]^{2}+\left[\cos\Theta(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{Z}^{*})\right]^{2}=\boldsymbol{I}_{k_{1}},

we have

\nu_{\min}^{2}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}\right)\geq 1-\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}},\boldsymbol{Z}^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\geq 1-\left(\frac{2c_{2}}{c_{1}}\delta\right)^{2}.

Hence,

\nu_{\min}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{Z}^{*}\right)\geq\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{2c_{2}}{c_{1}}\delta\right)^{2}}\geq 1-\frac{2c_{2}}{c_{1}}\delta.

Substituting this to (
35
) completes the proof.
∎

To prove Lemma
23
, we first show the following Lemma.

Lemma 24
.

Under Assumption
19
(a), there exists a universal constant
C
, such that

\mathbb{P}\left(\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|\geq C(\sqrt{k_{1}}+\sqrt{k_{2}}+\sqrt{t})\right)\leq 2\exp(-t).

Proof of Lemma
24
.

We can find an
\varepsilon
-net
\mathcal{M}
of the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{k_{1}-1}
and
\mathcal{N}
of the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{k_{2}-1}
with cardinalities:

|\mathcal{M}|\leq\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon}+1\right)^{k_{1}}\quad\text{ and }\quad|\mathcal{N}|\leq\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon}+1\right)^{k_{2}}.

Then the spectral norm of
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}
can be bounded using these nets as follows:

\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|\leq\frac{1}{1-2\varepsilon}\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathcal{N},\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathcal{M}}\langle\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\rangle,\quad\varepsilon\in\left[0,\frac{1}{2}\right)

(38)

Fix
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathcal{N},\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathcal{M}
. By the decomposition of
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}
in Assumption
19
(a)
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{1}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{2}}^{1/2}
, we have

\langle\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\rangle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{Z}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{2}}^{1/2}\cdot\boldsymbol{x}\right),\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{1}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{y}\right\rangle.

Denote
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{x}}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{2}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{x}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{y}}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{1}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{y}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\langle\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\rangle\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle\boldsymbol{Z}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{x}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{y}}\rangle\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}}\|Z_{ij}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{y}}_{j}\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq CK^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}}{\widetilde{x_{j}}}^{2}\widetilde{y}_{i}^{2}\leq CK^{2}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{x}}\|^{2}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{y}}\|^{2}\leq C\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{1}}\right\|\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{2}}\right\|,

where
K:=\max_{i,j}\|Z_{ij}\|_{\psi_{2}}
.
That leads to the tail bound:

\mathbb{P}(\langle\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\rangle\geq u)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{cu^{2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{1}}\right\|\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{2}}\right\|}\right).

By
\varepsilon
-net argument, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathcal{N},\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathcal{M}}\langle\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\rangle\geq u\right)\leq\sum_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathcal{N},\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathcal{M}}\mathbb{P}(\langle\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\rangle\geq u),

and setting
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{4}
, we bound the probability above by

9^{\left(k_{1}+k_{2}\right)}\cdot 2\exp\left(-\frac{cu^{2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{1}}\right\|\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{2}}\right\|}\right).

Choose
u=C\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{1}}^{1/2}\right\|\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{2}}^{1/2}\right\|\left(\sqrt{k_{2}}+\sqrt{k_{1}}+\sqrt{t}\right)
. If
C
is chosen sufficiently large, such that

\frac{cu^{2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{1}}\right\|\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{2}}\right\|}\geq 3\left(k_{1}+k_{2}\right)+t
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathcal{N},\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathcal{M}}\langle\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\rangle\geq u\right)\leq 9^{\left(k_{1}+k_{2}\right)}\cdot 2\exp\left(-3\left(k_{1}+k_{2}\right)-t\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-t\right).

Finally, combining the above inequality with (
38
) and by the assumption that
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{1}}\|
and
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{k_{2}}
are bounded, we conclude that

\mathbb{P}\left(\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|\geq C(\sqrt{k_{1}}+\sqrt{k_{2}}+\sqrt{t})\right)\leq 2\exp(-t).

∎

Proof of Lemma
23
.

We provide in details the proof for
\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{1}
as follows. The proof for
\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{2}
is similar and thus omitted here.
By definition, we can decompose
\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{1}
by

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{S}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{S}^{*}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{X}_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{i}^{\top}}-\frac{1}{p_{1}}\boldsymbol{R}^{*}{\boldsymbol{R}^{*}}^{\top}

(39)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\right)^{\top}-\frac{1}{p_{1}}\boldsymbol{R}^{*}{\boldsymbol{R}^{*}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{p_{1}}\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\left(\frac{1}{p_{2}n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{I}_{k_{1}}\right){\boldsymbol{R}^{*}}^{\top}}_{\boldsymbol{S}^{(1)}}+\underbrace{\left(\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{\top}-\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{U}^{(1)}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{S}^{(2)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}n}\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{\top}}_{\boldsymbol{S}^{(3)}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}n}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}(\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top})^{\top}}_{(\boldsymbol{S}^{(3)})^{\top}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{U}^{(1)}}_{\boldsymbol{S}^{(4)}},

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{U}^{(1)}:=\mathbb{E}{\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}}
. Now we establish the upper bounds of the operator norm of each terms above.

For
\boldsymbol{S}^{(1)}
, by Lemma
24
, there exist a constant
b
such that

\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{2}\leq b\left(\sqrt{k_{1}}+\sqrt{k_{2}}+\sqrt{t}\right)

with probability at lease
1-2e^{-t}
. In what follows, we analyze under this event.
We have,

\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\|_{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{2}\leq b^{\prime}\sqrt{p_{2}}\left(\sqrt{k_{1}}+\sqrt{k_{2}}+\sqrt{t}\right)

for some universal constant
b^{\prime}
. Accordingly,
\frac{1}{p_{2}}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}\|_{2}\leq b^{\prime\prime}t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})
for some universal constant
b^{\prime\prime}
. This indicates that
\frac{1}{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}
satisfies Bernstein condition with parameter
b^{\prime\prime}t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})
. Together with Assumption
19
(a) and by Bernstein inequality (Theorem 6.17 in
Wainwright (2019)
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{np_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{I}_{k}\right\|_{2}\geq\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{np_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{I}_{k}\right\|_{2}\geq\epsilon\;|\;\frac{1}{p_{2}}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}\|_{2}\leq b^{\prime\prime}t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{p_{2}}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}\|_{2}>b^{\prime\prime}t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2k_{1}\exp\left(-\frac{n\epsilon^{2}}{2b^{\prime\prime}t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})(1+\epsilon)}\right)+2e^{-t},

where we use that fact that

\displaystyle\var\left(\frac{1}{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{I}_{k_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}\right)^{2}\right]-\boldsymbol{I}_{k_{1}}

\displaystyle\preceq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\frac{1}{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}\right]-\boldsymbol{I}_{k_{i}}

\displaystyle\preceq b^{\prime\prime}t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}\right]-\boldsymbol{I}_{k_{1}}=[b^{\prime\prime}t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})-1]\boldsymbol{I}_{k_{1}}.

Then we have

\left\|\frac{1}{np_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{I}_{k}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{1}\sqrt{t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})}\sqrt{\frac{\log k_{1}+t}{n}}

with probability at lease
1-3e^{-t}
, and thus

\|\boldsymbol{S}^{(1)}\|_{2}\leq C_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{t(k_{1}\vee k_{2})}\sqrt{\frac{\log k_{1}+t}{n}}

with probability at lease
1-3e^{-t}
.

For
\boldsymbol{S}^{(2)}
, similarly to Lemma
24
, there exist a constant
b
such that
\|\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{\top}\|/(p_{1}p_{2})\leq bt(p_{1}\vee p_{2})/(p_{1}p_{2})
with probability at least
1-2e^{-t}
. Similar to the above part for
\boldsymbol{S}^{(1)}
, we have by Bernstein inequality,

\|\boldsymbol{S}^{(2)}\|_{2}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{t(p_{1}\vee p_{2})}{p_{1}p_{2}}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p_{1}+t}{n}}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}

with probability at least
1-3e^{-t}
.

Moreover,
\boldsymbol{S}^{(3)}
can be decomposed to

\|\boldsymbol{S}^{(3)}\|_{2}\leq\left\|\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}}\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}]\right\|_{2}+\left\|\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}]\right)\right\|_{2},

and the first term can be bounded by
C_{3}/\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}
by Assumption
5
. The second term is bounded using the same technique as above. First, we have

\|\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{\top}\|_{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\|_{2}\leq c_{3}\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}(\sqrt{k_{1}}+\sqrt{k_{2}}+\sqrt{p_{1}}+\sqrt{p_{2}}+t)

with probability at least
1-4e^{-t}
.
Hence
\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}}\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}
satisfies Bernstein condition with parameter
c_{3}^{\prime}{\frac{\sqrt{k_{1}}+\sqrt{k_{2}}+\sqrt{p_{1}}+\sqrt{p_{2}}+t}{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}}}
with probability at least
1-4e^{-t}
. Therefore, by Bernstein inequality, we have

\left\|\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}{\boldsymbol{C}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}^{\top}]\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{\sqrt{k_{1}}+\sqrt{k_{2}}+\sqrt{p_{1}}+\sqrt{p_{2}}+t}{p_{1}p_{2}}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p_{1}+t}{n}}

with probability at least
1-5e^{-t}
. Thus,
\|\boldsymbol{S}^{(3)}\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{3}}{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}}+C_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{\log p_{1}+t}{np_{1}p_{2}}}
with probability at least
1-5e^{-t}
.

Finally, for
\boldsymbol{S}^{(4)}
, we take use of Assumption
19
(b), which leads to

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{U}^{(1)}\right\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{p_{1}}\left(\mathbb{E}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{2}}u_{ij}^{2}\right)^{2}+\sum_{i,k\in[p_{1}]\\
i\neq k}\left(\mathbb{E}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{2}}u_{ij}u_{kj}\right)^{2}\leq p_{1}p_{2}^{2}b^{4}+p_{1}p_{2}c_{4}.

Thus, we have
\|\boldsymbol{S}^{(4)}\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{4}}{\sqrt{p_{1}}}
.

All in all, substituting all the above results into (
39
) completes the proof.

∎

A straightforward corollary of Proposition
8
is stated as follows.

Corollary 25
.

Under the same conditions as Proposition
8
, we have

\|\widehat{\mathbb{F}}-\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}\|_{2}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n(k_{1}\wedge k_{2})}{p_{1}p_{2}}}\right).

D.2
Analysis of OLS after pre-trained diversification

Proposition 26

(OLS after pre-trained diversification)
.

Suppose that the projection matrices
\boldsymbol{W}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{W}_{2}
satisfy Definition
4
, and Assumption
21
,
20
, and
22
in Section
C
in the supplementary hold. Furthermore, assume that
\sqrt{n}\ll p_{1}p_{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{U}+{\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{p_{1}p_{2}})}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{d}{\cal M}{\cal N}\big(\boldsymbol{0},(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Phi}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top})^{-1},\boldsymbol{\Psi}\big)

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}
,
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}
,
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
, and
\boldsymbol{\psi}
are defined in Assumption
21
,
20
, and
22
in Section
C
in the supplementary.

Before providing the proof of Proposition
26
, we first present two lemmas whose proofs will be given shortly after.

Lemma 27
.

Let
\widehat{\mathbb{F}}:=\big(\operatorname{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{1}),\ldots,\operatorname{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{n})\big)^{\top}
from the “pre-trained projection”.
Suppose Assumption
6
(b),
20
(b), and
22
(a) - (c) hold, then we have

\plim_{n,p_{1},p_{2}\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H},

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}:=\plim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{f}_{i}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{f}_{i}^{\top}
is positive definite,
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{f}}_{i}:=\vect(\boldsymbol{F}_{i})
, and
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}:=\plim_{p_{1},p_{2}\rightarrow\infty}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})
is full rank as been assumed in Assumption
22
(b) and Assumption
20
(b), respectively.

Lemma 28
.

Suppose the same condition of Lemma
27
holds, we have

\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}{\left[{\mathbb{F}}-\widehat{\mathbb{F}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\right]=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left({\frac{1}{\sqrt{np_{1}p_{2}}}}+\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}}\right).}

Proof of Proposition
26
.

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\mathbb{X}

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\left[\mathring{\mathbb{F}}\mathring{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{\top}+\mathbb{U}\right]

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\left[\widehat{\mathbb{F}}-\widehat{\mathbb{F}}+\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}\right]\mathring{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{\top}+\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\mathbb{U}

\displaystyle=\mathring{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{\top}+\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\left[{\mathbb{F}}-\widehat{\mathbb{F}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\right]{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{*\top}

\displaystyle+\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\left[\widehat{\mathbb{F}}-\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}\right]^{\top}\mathbb{U}+\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{U}

\displaystyle=:\mathring{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{\top}+{I}+{II}+{III}.

Lemma
28
shows that
{I}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left({\frac{1}{\sqrt{np_{1}p_{2}}}}+\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}}\right).

Also, for part
{II},
for every
r\in[p_{1}\!p_{2}]
, let
\mathbb{U}_{\cdot\,r}
be the
r
-th column of
\mathbb{U}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\left[\widehat{\mathbb{F}}-\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}\right]^{\top}\mathbb{U}_{\cdot\,r}=\frac{1}{p_{1}\!p_{2}}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{U}^{\top}\mathbb{U}_{\cdot\,r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[n\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\big]\big[\frac{1}{np_{1}\!p_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{1}\!p_{2}}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top})_{j}\widetilde{u}_{ij}\widetilde{u}_{ir}\big]=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{np_{1}p_{2}}}),

where the last identity follows from Lemma
27
and Assumption
22
(c). As for part
{III}
, by Assumption
22
(b), (d), Lemma
27
, and Slutsky Theorem, we have

\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{U}\xrightarrow{d}{\cal M}{\cal N}\big(\boldsymbol{0},(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Phi}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top})^{-1},\boldsymbol{\Psi}\big).

Combining all the three parts gives the conclusion.
∎

Proof of Lemma
27
.

By equation (
5
) and (
7
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{np_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{X}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{np_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}\mathbb{F}^{\top}+\mathbb{U}^{\top})(\mathbb{F}{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}^{\top}+\mathbb{U})(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{np_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{F}{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})+\frac{1}{np_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{U}^{\top}\mathbb{F}{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{np_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{*}\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{U}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})+\frac{1}{np_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{U}^{\top}\mathbb{U}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}+\frac{1}{np_{1}p_{2}}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{U}^{\top}\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{np_{1}p_{2}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{U}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})+\frac{1}{np_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{U}^{\top}\mathbb{U}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})

\displaystyle=:{I}+{II}+{III}+{IV}.

By Assumption
20
(b),
22
(b), and the continuous mapping theorem we know
{I}\xrightarrow{P}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}
.
Moreover, Assumption
22
(a) indicates that
{II},{III}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}o_{p}(1)
, and Assumption
22
(c) indicates that
{IV}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}o_{p}(1)
. Combining all the four parts gives the conclusion.
∎

Proof of Lemma
28
.

\displaystyle\left[{\mathbb{F}}-\widehat{\mathbb{F}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\right]^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}

(40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[{\mathbb{F}}-\widehat{\mathbb{F}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\right]^{\top}\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}+\left[{\mathbb{F}}-\widehat{\mathbb{F}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\right]^{\top}\left[\widehat{\mathbb{F}}-\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{p_{1}\!p_{2}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{U}^{\top}\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(p_{1}\!p_{2})^{2}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{U}^{\top}\mathbb{U}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{p_{1}\!p_{2}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{1}\!p_{2}}[\boldsymbol{W}_{2}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}]_{j}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{f}_{i}}^{\top}\widetilde{u}_{ij}\right](\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(p_{1}\!p_{2})^{2}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{1}\!p_{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{p_{1}\!p_{2}}[\boldsymbol{W}_{2}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}]_{j}[\boldsymbol{W}_{2}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}]_{k}^{\top}\widetilde{u}_{ij}\widetilde{u}_{ik}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{p_{1}\!p_{2}}\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(\sqrt{p_{1}\!p_{2}n})\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)-\frac{1}{(p_{1}\!p_{2})^{2}}\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(np_{1}\!p_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{p_{1}\!p_{2}}}+\frac{n}{p_{1}\!p_{2}}\right),

where the second identity is by substituting all
\widehat{\mathbb{F}}
by

\widehat{\mathbb{F}}=\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}+\frac{1}{p_{1}\!p_{2}}\mathbb{U}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})

(41)

according to equation (
6
), and the last identity is by Assumption
22
(a) - (c) and Law of Large Number.

Moreover, Lemma
27
indicates that
\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(n^{-1})
. The conclusion follows thereafter.
∎

D.3
Proof of Theorem
9

Proof.

By Proposition
26
, we have that

\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}=\mathring{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{M}}\mathbb{U}+\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{np_{1}p_{2}}}+\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}}),

where
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{M}}:=\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})\mathbb{F}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}\!k_{2}\times n}
.
Then

\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}=\frac{1}{p_{2}k_{2}}\boldsymbol{E}_{p_{1}p_{2}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}_{k_{1}k_{2}}^{\top}=\mathring{c}_{s}\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}+\frac{1}{p_{2}k_{2}}\boldsymbol{E}_{p_{1}p_{2}}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{M}}\mathbb{U})^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}_{k_{1}k_{2}}^{\top}+\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{np_{1}p_{2}}}+\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}}),

(42)

and by the proof of Proposition
26
,

\sqrt{n}\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{M}}\mathbb{U}\right)^{\top}\xrightarrow{\ d\ }{\cal M}{\cal N}\big(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{\Psi},(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Phi}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top})^{-1}\big).

By linear transformation of matrix Gaussian distribution, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{p_{2}k_{2}}}\boldsymbol{E}_{p_{1}p_{2}}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{M}}\mathbb{U})^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}_{k_{1}k_{2}}^{\top}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\ d\ }{\cal M}{\cal N}\big(\boldsymbol{0},\frac{1}{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{E}_{p_{1}p_{2}}\boldsymbol{\Psi}\boldsymbol{E}_{p_{1}p_{2}}^{\top},\frac{1}{k_{2}}\boldsymbol{E}_{k_{1}k_{2}}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Phi}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top})^{-1}\boldsymbol{E}_{k_{1}k_{2}}^{\top}\big).

Since
\frac{1}{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{E}_{p_{1}p_{2}}\boldsymbol{\Psi}\boldsymbol{E}_{p_{1}p_{2}}^{\top}=\frac{1}{p_{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{ii}+\sum_{i\neq j}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{ij}\right)\rightarrow\frac{1}{p_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{ii}
as
p_{2}
goes to infinity, by Assumption
22
(d), we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{p_{2}k_{2}}}\boldsymbol{E}_{p_{1}p_{2}}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{M}}\mathbb{U})^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}_{k_{1}k_{2}}^{\top}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\ d\ }{\cal M}{\cal N}\big(\boldsymbol{0},\frac{1}{p_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{p_{2}}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{ii},\frac{1}{k_{2}}\boldsymbol{E}_{k_{1}k_{2}}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Phi}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{H}^{\top})^{-1}\boldsymbol{E}_{k_{1}k_{2}}^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle={\cal M}{\cal N}\big(\boldsymbol{0},\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)_{p_{1}\times p_{1}},\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)_{k_{1}\times k_{1}}\big).

Bring back to equation (
42
), we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{np_{2}k_{2}}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}-\mathring{c}_{s}\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{n}{p_{2}k_{2}}}\boldsymbol{E}_{p_{1}p_{2}}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{M}}\mathbb{U})^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}_{k_{1}k_{2}}^{\top}+\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{k_{2}}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{nk_{2}}}{p_{1}\sqrt{p_{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\ d\ }{\cal M}{\cal N}\big(\boldsymbol{0},\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)_{p_{1}\times p_{1}},\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)_{k_{1}\times k_{1}}\big).

With some linear shuffle transformation, we can similarly get that

\displaystyle\sqrt{np_{1}k_{1}}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathring{r}_{s}\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}\right)\xrightarrow{\ d\ }{\cal M}{\cal N}\big(\boldsymbol{0},\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)_{p_{2}\times p_{2}},\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)_{k_{2}\times k_{2}}\big).

∎

D.4
Proof of Theorem
10

Proof.

For clear presentation of the proof, we assume that
\mathring{c}_{s}=1
and let
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}=\boldsymbol{X}_{i}-\widehat{}\boldsymbol{R}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{C}/\widehat{r}_{s}
. The proof is similar under the general setting without this assumption but more tedious.
By Theorem
9
, we obtain that

\sqrt{np_{2}k_{2}}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}\right)\xrightarrow{\ d\ }{\cal M}{\cal N}\left(\boldsymbol{0},\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)_{p_{1}\times p_{1}},\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)_{k_{1}\times k_{1}}\right),

which implies that
\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{i\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{i\cdot}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{{\sqrt{np_{2}}}}\right)
, where
\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{i\cdot}
and
\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{i\cdot}
are columns of the
i
-th row of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}
and
\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}
, respectively.
Also, it indicates that element-wise, we have

\sqrt{np_{2}k_{2}}\left(\widehat{r}_{ij}-\mathring{r}_{ij}\right)\xrightarrow{\ d\ }{\cal N}(0,b_{ij}),\ \text{\ for }i\in[p_{1}]\text{ and }j\in[k_{1}],

where
b_{ij}
are some constants. Thus,
|\widehat{r}_{ij}-\mathring{r}_{ij}|=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1/\sqrt{np_{2}})
.
Besides, Theorem
9
implies that
{\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{i\cdot}-\mathring{r}_{s}\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{i\cdot}\right\|_{2}}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{np_{1}}}\right)
.
Hence, we have

{\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{i\cdot}-\mathring{}\boldsymbol{C}_{i\cdot}\right\|_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\mathring{r}_{s}}\left(\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathring{r}_{s}\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|_{2}+|\widehat{r}_{s}-r_{s}|\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|_{2}\right)=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{np_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{np_{2}}}\right).

Now we can bound
|\widehat{u}_{i,jk}-u_{i,jk}|
as follows.

\displaystyle|\widehat{u}_{i,jk}-u_{i,jk}|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot})^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}-(\boldsymbol{R}^{*}_{j\cdot})^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}_{k\cdot}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot})^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}-(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{R}_{j\cdot})^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right|+\left|(\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot})^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{i}\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}-(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{R}_{j\cdot})^{\top}\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right|

\displaystyle+\left|(\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot})^{\top}\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}_{k\cdot}-(\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot})^{\top}\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}_{k\cdot}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot})^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{C}_{k\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{R}_{j\cdot}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\right\|_{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{C}_{k\cdot}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{np_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{np_{2}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}}\right)

∎

Appendix E
FAMAR-LowRank with Observed
\boldsymbol{F}_{t}

We first establish the results of the general optimization problem (
3
) with
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}
and
\boldsymbol{U}_{i}
known.
We start with introducing some notations. Let
\boldsymbol{G}_{i}:=\operatorname{diag}(\boldsymbol{F}_{i},\boldsymbol{U}_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{(k_{1}+p_{1})\times(k_{2}+p_{2})}
be the block-diagonal matrix of observations for
i\in[n]
. Let
\boldsymbol{\Theta}:=\operatorname{diag}(\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{B})\in\mathbb{R}^{(k_{1}+p_{1})\times(k_{2}+p_{2})}
be the block-diagonal matrix of matrix parameters. Then, the operator
\mathcal{G}_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{(k_{1}+p_{1})\times(k_{2}+p_{2})}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
is defined via
[\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})]_{i}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{G}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Theta}\right\rangle
. Similarly, define mapping
\mathcal{F}:\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}\times k_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
via
[\mathcal{F}(\boldsymbol{A})]_{i}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\right\rangle
and mapping
\mathcal{U}:\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
via
[\mathcal{U}(\boldsymbol{B})]_{i}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\right\rangle
.
With these notations, the MFR model (
1
) can be re-written as

\boldsymbol{y}=\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{F}(\boldsymbol{A}^{*})+\mathcal{U}(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},

where
\boldsymbol{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})^{\top}
,
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=(\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n})^{\top}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Theta^{*}}=\operatorname{diag}(\boldsymbol{A}^{*},\boldsymbol{B}^{*})
is the true parameter matrix. In addition, define the operator
\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{(k_{1}+p_{1})\times(k_{2}+p_{2})}
by
\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\boldsymbol{G}_{i}
.
Moreover, considering the nuclear norm regularization on
\boldsymbol{B}
, the following notations are needed.
For any true
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}
, let
r:=\operatorname{rank}(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})
. Matrix
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}
has a singular value decomposition of the form
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}=\boldsymbol{V}_{1}\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{V}_{2}^{\top}
, where
\boldsymbol{V}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{1}}
and
\boldsymbol{V}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times p_{2}}
are orthonormal matrices. Let
\boldsymbol{V}_{1}^{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times r}
and
\boldsymbol{V}_{2}^{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r}
be matrices of singular vectors associated with the top
r
singular values of
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}
. Two sub-spaces of
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}
are defined by

\mathcal{A}\left(\boldsymbol{V}_{1}^{r},\boldsymbol{V}_{2}^{r}\right):=\left\{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}\mid\operatorname{row}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B})\subseteq\boldsymbol{V}_{2}^{r}\text{ and }\operatorname{col}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B})\subseteq\boldsymbol{V}_{1}^{r}\right\}

and

\mathcal{B}\left(\boldsymbol{V}_{1}^{r},\boldsymbol{V}_{2}^{r}\right):=\left\{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}\mid\operatorname{row}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B})\perp\boldsymbol{V}_{2}^{r}\text{ and }\operatorname{col}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B})\perp\boldsymbol{V}_{1}^{r}\right\},

where row
(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B})\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}}
and
\operatorname{col}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B})\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}
are the row space and column space, respectively, of the matrix
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}
. The shorthand notations
\mathcal{A}^{r}
and
\mathcal{B}^{r}
are used when
\left(\boldsymbol{V}_{1}^{r},\boldsymbol{V}_{2}^{r}\right)
are already clear from the context.

With that, we are able to introduce an important technical condition called adjusted-restricted strong convexity condition (adjusted-RSC), which is a modification of the strong convexity condition (RSC) proposed by
Negahban & Wainwright (2011)
.
Letting
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{(k_{1}+p_{1})\times(k_{2}+p_{2})}
denote the restricted set, we say that the operator
\mathcal{G}_{n}
satisfies adjusted-RSC over the set
\mathcal{C}
if there exists some
\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})>0
such that

\frac{1}{2n}\|\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|^{2}\geq\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}\ \text{ for all }\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{C}.

To specify the set of interest, let
\Pi_{\mathcal{B}^{r}}
denote the projection operator onto the subspace
\mathcal{B}^{r}
, and let

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}=\Pi_{\mathcal{B}^{r}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}),\quad\text{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}.

(43)

The set of low-rank matrices is defined by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}:=

\displaystyle\left\{\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}&\bf 0\\
\bf 0&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\end{pmatrix}\mid\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}\times k_{2}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}},\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\|_{*}\leq 3\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{*}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}\|_{*}\right\}.

The following theorem presents the Frobenius norm error bound for the FARMA estimator.

Theorem 29
.

Suppose that the operator
\mathcal{G}_{n}
satisfies adjusted-RSC with constant
\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})>0
over the set
\mathcal{C}
, and that the tuning parameter
\lambda_{n}
is chosen such that
\lambda_{n}\geq 2\|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\|_{2}/n
. Then any solution
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}:=\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A}&\bf 0\\
\bf 0&\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\end{pmatrix}
to (
3
) satisfies

\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|_{F}\leq\frac{\lambda_{n}}{\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})}\big(2\sqrt{k_{1}\wedge k_{2}}+6\sqrt{2\operatorname{rank}(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})}\big).

Remark 7
.

By taking
\lambda_{n}\asymp\big\|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\big\|_{2}/n
, Theorem
29
indicates that

\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|_{F}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\frac{\big\|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\big\|_{2}}{n\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})}\right)

with
k_{1}\asymp k_{1}\asymp\rank(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\asymp 1
.
The following proposition provides the convergence rate under this case with sub-Gaussian assumptions.

Assumption 30
.

For
i\in[n]
, assume that

(i)

\mathbb{E}\varepsilon_{i}\boldsymbol{G}_{i}=\boldsymbol{0}
;

(ii)

\varepsilon_{i}
is
\sigma
-sub-Gaussian, and there exists a constant
N
such that
\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\boldsymbol{G}_{i}\|\leq N
.

Proposition 31
.

Suppose that the operator
\mathcal{G}_{n}
satisfies adjusted-RSC with constant
\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})>0
over the set
\mathcal{C}
, and that the tuning parameter
\lambda_{n}
is chosen such that
\lambda_{n}\asymp 2\|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\|_{2}/n
. In addition, suppose Assumption
30
holds and
k_{1}\asymp k_{1}\asymp\rank(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\asymp 1
.
Then we have

\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|_{F}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(k_{1}+k_{2}+p_{1}+p_{2})}{n}}\right).

E.1
Proof of Theorem
29

We start with providing a Lemma on the estimation error.

Lemma 32
.

Let
\boldsymbol{V}_{1}^{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times r}
, and
\boldsymbol{V}_{2}^{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r}
be matrices consisting of the top
r
left and right (respectively) singular vectors of
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}
. Then there exists
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}
of the error
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}
such that:

(a)

\operatorname{rank}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime})\leq 2r
;

(b)

if
\lambda_{n}\geq 2\left\|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\right\|_{2}/n
, then the nuclear norm of
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}
is bounded as

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{*}\leq 3\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\right\|_{*}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}\right\|_{*}

The proof of the Lemma will be provided shortly after, and with that, we are ready to prove Theorem
29
.

Proof of Theorem
29
.

Denote
\widetilde{\operatorname{vec}}(\boldsymbol{G}_{i})=(\operatorname{vec}(\boldsymbol{F}_{i})^{\top},\operatorname{vec}(\boldsymbol{U}_{i})^{\top})^{\top}
and
\widetilde{\operatorname{vec}}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})=(\operatorname{vec}(\boldsymbol{A})^{\top},\operatorname{vec}(\boldsymbol{B})^{\top})^{\top}
. Then
[\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})]_{i}=\widetilde{\operatorname{vec}}(\boldsymbol{G}_{i})^{\top}\widetilde{\operatorname{vec}}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})
. By optimality, we have

\frac{1}{2n}\big\|\boldsymbol{y}-\mathcal{G}_{n}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}})\big\|^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}\|_{*}\leq\frac{1}{2n}\big\|\boldsymbol{y}-\mathcal{G}_{n}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{*})\big\|^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{B}}^{*}\|_{*}.

Letting
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}:=\boldsymbol{B}^{*}-\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}
,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}:=\boldsymbol{A}^{*}-\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}:=\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\|\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\right\rangle+\lambda_{n}\big(\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}-\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\big)

(44)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\big|\left\langle\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}),\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right\rangle\big|+\lambda_{n}\big(\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}-\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{*}+\lambda_{n}\big(\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}-\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\big),

where the first identity is by definition, and the second inequality is by Hölder’s inequality. Lemma
32
(b) shows that
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{C}
. Thus, by adjusted-RSC, we have

\frac{1}{2n}\|\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|^{2}\geq\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}.

(45)

Combining inequality (
44
) and (
45
) gives

\displaystyle\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2n}\|\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{*}+\lambda_{n}\big(\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}-\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{n}\big(\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{*}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{n}\big(\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}\|_{*}+\frac{3}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}\big)

where the third inequality is by the condition that
\lambda_{n}\geq\frac{2}{n}\|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\|_{2}
and the triangular inequality. By triangular inequality and Lemma
32
, we have

\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{*}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\|_{*}\leq 4\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{*}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}\|_{*}

which leads to

\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\lambda_{n}\big(2\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}\|_{*}+6\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{*}\big).

The proof is completed by the fact that for any matrix
\boldsymbol{D}
,
\|\boldsymbol{D}\|_{*}\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{rank}(\boldsymbol{D})}\|\boldsymbol{D}\|_{F}
, which leads to

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{n}}{\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})}\big(2\sqrt{k_{1}\wedge k_{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}\|_{F}+6\sqrt{2\operatorname{rank}(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{F}\big)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{n}}{\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})}\big(2\sqrt{k_{1}\wedge k_{2}}+6\sqrt{2\operatorname{rank}(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})}\big)\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
32
.

Part (a) of the Lemma was proved in
Recht et al. (2010)
and
Negahban & Wainwright (2011)
. The proof is provided here for completeness. Recall that the SVD of
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}
is
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}=\boldsymbol{V}_{1}\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{V}_{2}^{\top}
,
\boldsymbol{V}_{1}^{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times r}
(resp.
\boldsymbol{V}_{2}^{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r}
) is the matrix of singular vectors associated with the top
r
left (resp. right) singular values of
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{H}=\boldsymbol{V}_{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\boldsymbol{V}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}
, and write it in block form as

\boldsymbol{H}=\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{H}_{11}&\boldsymbol{H}_{12}\\
\boldsymbol{H}_{21}&\boldsymbol{H}_{22}\end{pmatrix},\text{ where }\boldsymbol{H}_{11}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}\text{ and }\boldsymbol{H}_{22}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p_{1}-r)\times(p_{2}-r)}.

Let
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}:=\boldsymbol{V}_{1}\begin{pmatrix}\bf 0&\bf 0\\
\bf 0&\boldsymbol{H}_{22}\end{pmatrix}\in{\cal B}(\boldsymbol{V}_{1}^{r},\boldsymbol{V}_{2}^{r}
), and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime}:=\boldsymbol{\Delta}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}
.
Note that

\rank(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime})=\rank\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{H}_{11}&\boldsymbol{H}_{12}\\
\boldsymbol{H}_{21}&\bf 0\end{pmatrix}\leq\rank\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{H}_{11}&\boldsymbol{H}_{12}\\
\bf 0&\bf 0\end{pmatrix}+\rank\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{H}_{11}&\bf 0\\
\boldsymbol{H}_{21}&\bf 0\end{pmatrix}\leq 2r.

For part (b), we have derived in (
44
) that

0\leq\frac{1}{2n}\|\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{*}+\lambda_{n}\big(\|\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\|_{*}-\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\big).

(46)

By construction of
{\cal A}^{r}
and
{\cal B}^{r}
, we know
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}=\Pi_{{\cal B}^{r}}(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})
, and

\left\|\boldsymbol{B}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{*}=\|\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\|_{*}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\|_{*}.

Hence

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\right\|_{*}=\left\|\boldsymbol{B}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\right\|_{*}\geq\left\|\boldsymbol{B}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{*}-\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\right\|_{*}=\|\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\|_{*}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\|_{*}-\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{*}.

Thus

\|\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\|_{*}-\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{*}-\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\|_{*}.

Substituting that to (
46
), we have

0\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{*}+\lambda_{n}\big(\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{*}-\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\|_{*}\big).

Since
\lambda_{n}\geq 2\|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\|_{2}/n
, that leads to

0\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{*}-\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\|_{*}+\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{*}=\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{*}-\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\|_{*}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}\|_{*}\leq\frac{3}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{*}-\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\|_{*}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}\|_{*},

i.e.

\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime\prime}\|_{*}\leq 3\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}^{\prime}\|_{*}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}\|_{*}.

∎

E.2
Proof of Proposition
31

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{Q}_{i}:=\diag(\boldsymbol{G}_{i},\boldsymbol{G}_{i}^{\top})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, where
d:=k_{1}+k_{2}+p_{1}+p_{2}
. Then
\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{i}\|_{2}=\|\boldsymbol{G}_{i}\|_{2}
, and by the assumptions,
\varepsilon_{i}\boldsymbol{Q}_{i}
is sub-Gaussian with parameter
\boldsymbol{V}=c^{2}\sigma^{2}N^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{d}
, where
c
is some universal constant. Hence, by Matrix Hoeffding’s inequality, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\right\|\geq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\right)\leq d^{-2}

(47)

for
C_{1}
large enough. The conclusion follows directly from Theorem
29
.
∎

Appendix F
FAMAR-LowRank with Estimated
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}

Let
\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{y}:=(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\widehat{\mathbb{P}})\boldsymbol{y}
denote the residuals of the response vector
\boldsymbol{y}
after projecting onto the column space of
\widehat{\mathbb{F}}
, where
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}:=\widehat{\mathbb{F}}(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}})^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}
is the corresponding projection matrix. Then
\widehat{\mathbb{U}}=(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\widehat{\mathbb{P}})\mathbb{X}
. Thus,
\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{U}}=\boldsymbol{0}
, and it is straightforward to verify that the solution of (
3
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\in\argmin_{\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}}\left\{\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\widetilde{y}_{i}-\left\langle\boldsymbol{B},\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{i}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\right\},

\displaystyle\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A})=\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}.

F.1
Consistency of
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A}

Proof of Theorem
12
Part I.

Let
\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}:=\boldsymbol{A}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{R}^{*}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}\times k_{2}}
,
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Phi}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{*}:=(\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}\times k_{2}}
.
Vectorizing them gives

\displaystyle\vect(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*})

\displaystyle=\vect(\boldsymbol{A}^{*})-(\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\otimes\boldsymbol{R}^{*})^{\top}\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*}),

\displaystyle\vect(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Phi}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{*})

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\vect(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}).

Recall that the true MFR (
1
) is

y_{i}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{A}^{*},\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\right\rangle+\left\langle\boldsymbol{B}^{*},\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\right\rangle+\varepsilon_{i}.

Thus, we have

y_{i}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{F}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}\right\rangle+\left\langle\boldsymbol{X}_{i},\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\right\rangle+\varepsilon_{i}.

By the definition of
\widehat{\mathbb{U}}
and MFM (
1
), it can be equivalently rewritten as

\boldsymbol{y}={\mathbb{F}}\vect(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*})+\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{\top}+\widehat{\mathbb{U}}\big)\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},

where
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}:=\widehat{}\boldsymbol{C}\otimes\widehat{}\boldsymbol{R}
.
We are now ready to calculate the error of
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A}
by

\displaystyle\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A})-(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\vect(\boldsymbol{A}^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{F}}\vect(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*})+\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{\top}\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle-\left[(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\vect(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*})-(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\otimes\boldsymbol{R}^{*})^{\top}\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}{{\mathbb{F}}}-(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\right]\vect(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*})

\displaystyle+\left[\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}-(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{C}^{*}\otimes\boldsymbol{R}^{*})^{\top}\right]\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})+\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\left[\mathbb{F}-\widehat{\mathbb{F}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\right]\vect(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*})+\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\right)\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})+\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},

where the last identity uses the equality
\mathbb{F}=\widehat{\mathbb{F}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}+\left[\mathbb{F}-\widehat{\mathbb{F}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\right]
.
Applying the
\ell_{2}
-norm, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A})-(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\vect(\boldsymbol{A}^{*})\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\left[\mathbb{F}-\widehat{\mathbb{F}}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\right]\right\|\big\|\vect(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*})\big\|+\big\|(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\big\|+\big\|\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\big\|.

The first part, by Lemma
28
, under Assumption
6
(b),
20
(b), and
22
(a) - (c), can be bounded by
\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\big\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}\big\|_{F}/\sqrt{np_{1}p_{2}}\right)
. The second part, by the definition of operator norm and Proposition
26
, can be bounded by

\big\|(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\big\|\leq\|\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|_{1}\big\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\big\|_{2,1}\leq\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\|\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|_{1}\sqrt{\frac{(k_{1}\wedge k_{2})k_{1}k_{2}}{p_{1}p_{2}}}\right).

As for the last term, we have shown that under the condition of Lemma
27
,
\lambda_{\min}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}/n\big)^{1/2}\geq\Theta(1)
. Together with the fact that
\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\big\|=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(\sqrt{n})
, we have

\left\|\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{n}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}/n)}\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\big\|\leq\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

Combining the above results together, we get

\displaystyle\big\|\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A})-(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\vect(\boldsymbol{A}^{*})\big\|\leq\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\frac{\big\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}\big\|_{F}}{\sqrt{np_{1}p_{2}}}+\frac{\|\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|_{1}}{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

Moreover, by the assumption that
\|\boldsymbol{A}^{*}\|_{F}=O(p_{1}p_{2})
and
\|\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\|_{*}
is bounded (so
\|\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|_{1}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}})
), we complete the proof of the first part of the theorem by

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}-\boldsymbol{A}^{*}\right\|_{F}}{\|\boldsymbol{A}^{*}\|_{F}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\big\|\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A})-(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\vect(\boldsymbol{A}^{*})\big\|}{\|\boldsymbol{A}^{*}\|_{F}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\frac{\big\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}\big\|_{F}}{p_{1}p_{2}\sqrt{np_{1}p_{2}}}+\frac{\|\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|_{1}}{p_{1}p_{2}\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}}+\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{np_{1}p_{2}}}\right)

∎

F.2
Consistency of
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}

Proof of Theorem
12
Part II.

Replacing the true
(\boldsymbol{F}_{i},\boldsymbol{U}_{i})
in MFM (
1
) by the estimated
(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}.\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{i})
, we have

\displaystyle y_{i}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{F}_{i},\boldsymbol{A}^{*}\right\rangle+\left\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{i},\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\right\rangle+\varepsilon_{i}

\displaystyle=\left\langle(\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1},\boldsymbol{A}^{*}\right\rangle+\left\langle\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{i},\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\right\rangle+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\varepsilon$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}=\left\langle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}\right\rangle+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\varepsilon$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i},

where
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\varepsilon$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}:=y_{i}-\left\langle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}\right\rangle
and
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}=\operatorname{diag}(\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1})^{\top},\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{i})
.
Consider the optimization problem (
3
).
Let

\big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{A}$\kern 0.0pt}}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}\big)\in\argmin_{\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}\times k_{2}},\\
\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}}\left\{\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(y_{i}-\left\langle\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{i}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1})^{\top}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{B},\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{i}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\right\}.

(48)

Let
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}:=\diag(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{A}$\kern 0.0pt}}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}})
, and recall that
\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}=\operatorname{diag}(\boldsymbol{A}^{*},\boldsymbol{B}^{*})
and the operator
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{(k_{1}+p_{1})\times(k_{2}+p_{2})}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is defined such that
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})=\left\langle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Theta}\right\rangle
.
Letting
\boldsymbol{\Delta}:={\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{*}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}
,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}:=\boldsymbol{B}^{*}-\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}
, we have
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{F}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}
.
Optimality of

\boldsymbol{\Theta}

gives

\frac{1}{2n}\big\|\boldsymbol{y}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}})\big\|^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}\|_{*}\leq\frac{1}{2n}\big\|\boldsymbol{y}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{*})\big\|^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{B}}^{*}\|_{*}=\frac{1}{2n}\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\|^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{B}}^{*}\|_{*}.

By elementary algebra, we get

\frac{1}{2n}\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}$\kern 0.0pt}}},\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\right\rangle+\lambda_{n}\big(\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}-\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\big).

(49)

Based on the proof of Theorem
29
and Lemma
32
, to upper bound the error of
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}
, we only need to show that all the regularization conditions in Theorem
29
hold for optimization problem (
48
) with respect to the error
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
and the residual
{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
.

There are two parts to be verified. The first is to show that the error
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{C}
, where the set
\mathcal{C}
is defined following Definition
11
, with the projections of
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{b}
defined based on the SVD of
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}
, the lower-right part of
\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}
.
This is straightforward from Lemma
32
and the condition that
\lambda_{n}\geq 2\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}({{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}$\kern 0.0pt}}}}})\|_{2}/n
, where
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}$\kern 0.0pt}}})
, as a reminder, is defined as
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\varepsilon$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}
.

What left is to show that the adjust-RSC holds for
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}
with some constant
{\kappa}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n})
for all matrices in
\mathcal{C}
based on the assumption that
\mathcal{G}_{n}
satisfied adjusted-RSC with constant
\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})
over the set
\mathcal{C}
. That is, to show for all
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{C}
,

\|\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|_{2}^{2}/(2n)\geq\kappa_{2}(\mathcal{G}_{n})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}\Rightarrow\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|_{2}^{2}/(2n)\geq\kappa(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2},\text{ for some }\kappa(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}).

By triangular inequality,

\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|_{2}\geq\|\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|_{2}-\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|_{2}.

By the definition of
\mathcal{G}_{n}
and Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\langle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{G}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}

(50)

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{G}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

By proof of Theorem
10
, we have

\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{np_{2}}}\right),\ \text{ and }\ \left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{2}}{np_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

Moreover, by the Assumption
19
, we know that for any
j\in[k_{1}]
and
\ell\in[k_{2}]
,
F_{1,j\ell},\ldots,F_{n,j\ell}
are independent sub-Gaussian random variables, where
F_{i,j\ell}
is the
(j,\ell)
-th element of
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}
. Therefore,
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}F_{i,j\ell}^{2}
is sub-Exponential. By Maximal tail inequality, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{j,\ell}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}F_{i,j\ell}^{2}\geq C\log k_{1}k_{2}+Cu\right)\leq e^{-u}.

Thus,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}(1)
.
With the assumption
\|\boldsymbol{R}\|_{2}=O(p_{1})
and
\|\boldsymbol{C}\|_{2}=O(p_{2})
, by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we obtain the following bound.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{R}^{*}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{C}^{*\top}\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 7\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}-\mathring{c}_{s}\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathring{r}_{s}\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+7\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+7\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+7\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+7\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+7\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+7\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{n}+\frac{p_{2}}{n}+1\right)

Together with inequality (
50
) and Proposition
8
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|_{2}^{2}}{n\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{G}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{F}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}-\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}\right]\leq C_{1}\left(\frac{p_{1}\vee p_{2}}{n}+1\right)

for some constant
C_{1}
.
Together with the assumption
\frac{p_{1}\vee p_{2}}{n}<C_{0}
and that
\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})>2C_{1}(C_{0}+1)
, we have

\frac{\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|_{2}^{2}}{n\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}}\geq\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})-C_{1}(C_{0}+1)\geq\frac{\kappa(\mathcal{G}_{n})}{2}=:\kappa(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}).

Finally, we can prove following the idea in the proof of Theorem
29
, as briefly outlined the the follows. From inequality (
49
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}$\kern 0.0pt}}},\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\right\rangle+\lambda_{n}\big(\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}-\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}$\kern 0.0pt}}}),\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right\rangle+\lambda_{n}\big(\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}-\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}$\kern 0.0pt}}})\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{*}+\lambda_{n}\big(\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}-\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{n}\big(\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{*}}{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}\big)=\lambda_{n}\big(\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}\|_{*}+\frac{3}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}\big),

where the last inequality is by the assumption that
\lambda_{n}\geq 2\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}$\kern 0.0pt}}})\|_{2}/n
, and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{A}
is defined as
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{A}$\kern 0.0pt}}}
.
Combining the above bound with the fact that
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{C}
and
\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|_{2}^{2}/(2n)\geq\kappa(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}
, we derive that
