\|\boldsymbol{B}^{*}-\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}\leq\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}-{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{*}\|_{F}\leq\frac{2\lambda_{n}}{\kappa_{2}(\mathcal{G}_{n})}\big(2\sqrt{k_{1}\wedge k_{2}}+6\sqrt{2\operatorname{rank}(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})}\big).

∎

F.3
Proof of Proposition
13

In cases where FAMAR simplifies to matrix regression with MFM covariates (
2
), specifically when
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}=\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\boldsymbol{C}
, it becomes possible to eliminate
\bar{}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
and directly utilize the true error
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
in determining the tuning parameter
\lambda_{n}
.

Corollary 33
.

Suppose the assumptions inTheorem
12
hold.
When
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}=\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\boldsymbol{C}
, when
\lambda_{n}\geq 2\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\|_{2}/n
, the result for
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\|_{F}
still holds.

Proof of Corollary
33
.

Consider the same optimization problem as (
3
):

\big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{A}$\kern 0.0pt}}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}\big)\in\argmin_{\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}\times k_{2}},\\
\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}}\left\{\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(y_{i}-\left\langle\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{i}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1})^{\top}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{B},\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{i}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\right\}.

(51)

Let
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}:=\diag(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{A}$\kern 0.0pt}}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}})
.
Different from the proof of Theorem
12
, we define

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{*}=\operatorname{diag}(\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{C}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1})^{\top},\boldsymbol{B}^{*}).

Recall that
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}=\operatorname{diag}((\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1},\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{i})
, and the operator
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{(k_{1}+p_{1})\times(k_{2}+p_{2})}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is defined that
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})=\left\langle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Theta}\right\rangle
. By the decomposition
\boldsymbol{X}_{i}=\widehat{}\boldsymbol{R}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{C}^{\top}+\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}
, we have

\varepsilon_{i}=y_{i}-\left\langle\boldsymbol{X}_{i},\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\right\rangle=y_{i}-\left\langle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{*}\right\rangle.

Letting
\boldsymbol{\Delta}:={\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}}^{*}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}
,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}:=\boldsymbol{B}^{*}-\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}
, we have
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{F}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{F}
.
Optimality of

\boldsymbol{\Theta}

gives

\frac{1}{2n}\big\|\boldsymbol{y}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}})\big\|^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}\|_{*}\leq\frac{1}{2n}\big\|\boldsymbol{y}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}({\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}}^{*})\big\|^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{B}}^{*}\|_{*}=\frac{1}{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{B}}^{*}\|_{*}.

By elementary algebra, we get

\frac{1}{2n}\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n}\left\langle{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},\mathcal{G}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\right\rangle+\lambda_{n}\big(\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{B}\|_{*}-\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{*}\big).

(52)

Similar to Theorem
12
, to upper bound the error of
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}
, we only need to show that all the regularization conditions in Theorem
29
hold for optimization problem (
48
) with respect to the error
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
and the residual
{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
.

Lemma
32
and the condition that
\lambda_{n}\geq 2\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}({{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}})\|_{2}/n
shows that
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{C}$\kern 0.0pt}}}
, where the set

\mathcal{C}

is defined following Definition
11
, with the projections of
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{b}
defined based on the SVD of
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}
, the lower-right part of
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{*}
.
It is straightforward to show that

\mathcal{C}

=
\mathcal{C}
, where

\mathcal{C}

is defined based on
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{*}
while
\mathcal{C}
is defined based on
\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}
, for both
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{*}
and
\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}
have the structure
\operatorname{diag}(*,\boldsymbol{B}^{*})
.
Thus, we have
\boldsymbol{\Delta}:={\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}}^{*}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\Theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\in\mathcal{C}
.

The rest of the proof is exactly the same as that for Theorem
12
by replacing

\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}

with
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
.

∎

Proof of Proposition
13
.

By taking
\lambda_{n}\asymp 2\big\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\big\|_{2}/n
, Corollary
33
indicates that

\frac{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\|_{F}}{\big\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}({\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\big\|_{2}/n}={\mathcal{O}_{p}}\left(1\right),

when all other quantities are finite.
By triangular inequality,

\big\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\big\|_{2}\leq\big\|{\mathcal{G}}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\big\|_{2}+\big\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})-{\mathcal{G}}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\big\|_{2}=\big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\boldsymbol{G}_{i}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{G}_{i})\big\|_{2}.

(53)

By inequality (
47
) in the proof of Proposition
31
, we already have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\mathcal{G}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\right\|\geq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\right)\leq d^{-2}

(54)

for
C_{1}
large enough.

For the second term, by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{G}_{i})\big\|_{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\varepsilon_{i}|\big\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{G}_{i}\big\|_{2}\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{G}_{i}\big\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

Since
\varepsilon_{i}
is
\sigma-
sub-Gaussian, we know
\varepsilon_{i}^{2}
is sub-exponential with parameters
(4\sqrt{2}\sigma^{2},\,4\sigma^{2})
, i.e.

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\lambda\varepsilon_{i}^{2}}\right]\leq e^{16\sigma^{4}\lambda^{2}},\ \forall|\lambda|<\frac{1}{4\sigma^{2}}.

Hence
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{2}/n
is sub-Exponential with parameters
(4\sqrt{2}\sigma^{2},\,4\sqrt{n}\sigma^{2})
, and therefore

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\geq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)\leq n^{-1}

(55)

for large enough
C_{2}
.
In addition, by Corollary
25
and Theorem
10
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{G}_{i}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{G}_{i}\big\|_{F}^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{F}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\big\|_{F}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{U}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{U}_{i}\big\|_{F}^{2}

(56)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(p_{1}p_{2}\big(\sqrt{\frac{n(k_{1}\wedge k_{2})k_{1}k_{2}}{p_{1}p_{2}}}+1\big)^{2}\right).

Plugging (
54
), (
55
), and (
56
) into (
53
), we get

\frac{\big\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\mathcal{G}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\big\|_{2}}{n}=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(k_{1}+k_{2}+p_{1}+p_{2})}{n}}+\frac{(\log n)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}}\sqrt{(k_{1}\wedge k_{2})k_{1}k_{2}}+\frac{(\log n)^{1/4}}{n^{3/4}}\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}\right),

where the second term is negligible.
The proof is completed by Corollary
33
.
∎

Appendix G
FAMAR-Sparse with Observed
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}

When the true
\boldsymbol{B}^{*}
is sparse, the results from
Fan, Ke & Wang (2020)
can be similarly applied by vectorizing the matrices and applying sparse regularization.
Here, we take Lasso as example.
Let
\boldsymbol{\theta}:=(\vect(\boldsymbol{B})^{\top},\vect(\boldsymbol{A})^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}p_{2}+K_{1}K_{2}}
, and
\boldsymbol{\beta}:=\boldsymbol{\theta}_{[p_{1}p_{2}]}=\vect(\boldsymbol{B})
.
Denote
S:=\supp(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
,
S_{1}=\supp(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})
, and
S_{2}=[p_{1}p_{2}+K_{1}K_{2}]\setminus S
.
Further, let
\boldsymbol{w}_{i}:=(\vect(\boldsymbol{U}_{i})^{\top},\vect(\boldsymbol{F}_{i})^{\top})^{\top}
,
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{w}_{i}:=(\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{i})^{\top},\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i})^{\top})^{\top}
, and
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}:=(\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{i})^{\top},\vect(\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top})^{-1})^{\top})^{\top}
,
Let
\boldsymbol{W}:=(\boldsymbol{w}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{w}_{n})^{\top}
,
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{W}:=(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{w}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{}\boldsymbol{w}_{n})^{\top}
, and
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}:=(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{1},\ldots,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n})^{\top}
.
By adding the subscript
S
, we only take the
j
-th column for all
j\in S
.
Define the loss function

L_{n}(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta})=\frac{1}{2n}\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}.

We solve

\big(\widehat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}^{*}\big)\in\argmin_{\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{B}}\left\{\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(y_{i}-\left\langle\boldsymbol{A},{\boldsymbol{F}}_{i}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{B},{\boldsymbol{U}}_{i}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|\vect(\boldsymbol{B})\|_{1}\right\}.

After vectorization, this equals to

\widehat{}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\in\argmin_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}p_{2}+K_{1}K_{2}}}\left\{L_{n}(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta})+\lambda_{n}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{[p_{1}p_{2}]}\|_{1}\right\},

where
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}=(\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top},\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A}^{*})^{\top})^{\top}
.

We make the following assumptions.

Assumption 34
.

All elements of
\boldsymbol{W}
are sub-Gaussian.

Proposition 35
.

(i)

Error bounds : Under Assumptions
14
, if

\lambda_{n}>\frac{7}{\tau}\left\|\frac{1}{n}(\boldsymbol{W}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W})\right\|_{\infty},

then
\operatorname{supp}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}})\subseteq S
and

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{3}{5\kappa_{\infty}}\left(\left\|\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{\infty}+\lambda_{n}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{2}{\kappa_{2}}\left(\left\|\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}+\lambda_{n}\sqrt{\left|S_{1}\right|}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\leq\min\left\{\frac{3}{5\kappa_{\infty}}\left(\left\|\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{1}+\lambda_{n}\left|S_{1}\right|\right),\frac{2\sqrt{|S|}}{\kappa_{2}}\left(\left\|\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}+\lambda_{n}\sqrt{\left|S_{1}\right|}\right)\right\}.

(ii)

Sign consistency : In addition, if the following condition

\displaystyle\min\left\{\left|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j}^{*}\right|:\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j}^{*}\neq 0,j\in[p]\right\}>\frac{C}{\kappa_{\infty}\tau}\left\|\nabla L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

holds for some
C\geq 5
, then by taking
\lambda\in\left(\frac{7}{\tau}\left\|\nabla L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{\infty},\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\frac{5C}{3}-1\right)\left\|\nabla L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\right)
, the estimator achieves the sign consistency
\operatorname{sign}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})=\operatorname{sign}\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}\right)
.

Proof of Proposition
35
.

Taking gradient w.r.t.
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{S}
, we get

\displaystyle\triangledown_{S}L_{n}(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta})=\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{y})\in\mathbb{R}^{|S|},

\displaystyle\triangledown_{SS}^{2}L_{n}(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta})=\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{|S|\times|S|},

\displaystyle\triangledown_{jS}^{2}L_{n}(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{ij}\boldsymbol{w}_{iS}.

Thus, for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}p_{1}+K_{1}K_{2}}
,
\|\triangledown_{\cdot S}^{2}L_{n}(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta})-\triangledown_{\cdot S}^{2}L_{n}(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\|_{\infty}=0.

Assumption 4.1 in
Fan, Ke & Wang (2020)
holds with
M=1
and
A=\infty
.
Assumption 4.2 and 4.3 in
Fan, Ke & Wang (2020)
are satisfied by Assumption
14
.
Therefore, by Theorem 4.1 in
Fan, Ke & Wang (2020)
, we get the results.
∎

Corollary 36
.

Suppose all the conditions in Proposition
35
hold, and moreover, Assumption
34
holds. Then, by taking
\lambda_{n}>\frac{7}{\tau}\left\|\frac{1}{n}(\boldsymbol{W}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W})\right\|_{\infty}
and
\lambda_{n}\asymp\frac{7}{\tau}\left\|\frac{1}{n}(\boldsymbol{W}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W})\right\|_{\infty}
,
we have
\operatorname{supp}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}})\subseteq S
and

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|S|}{n}}+\frac{(\log(dn))^{2}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|S|}{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{|S|}(\log(dn))^{2}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{|S|}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{{|S|}(\log(dn))^{2}}{n}\right),

where
d=p_{1}p_{2}+K_{1}K_{2}
.

Proof of Corollary
36
.

Recall that
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
. We have

\displaystyle\|\triangledown_{S}L_{n}(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\|_{\infty}=\frac{1}{n}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|S|}{n}}\right),

\displaystyle\|\triangledown_{S}L_{n}(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\|_{2}=\frac{1}{n}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}={\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|S|}{n}}\right)},

\displaystyle\|\triangledown_{S}L_{n}(\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{W}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\|_{1}=\frac{1}{n}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left({\frac{|S|}{\sqrt{n}}}\right).

We also have to determine the order of
\lambda_{n}
.
By taking
d=p_{1}p_{2}+K_{1}K_{2}
and by Assumption
34
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|w_{ij}|\|\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\|\leq t)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(|w_{ij}|\leq\sqrt{t},\|\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\|\leq\sqrt{t})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\|\leq\sqrt{t}\wedge C\sqrt{d})-\mathbb{P}(|w_{ij}|>\sqrt{t})

\displaystyle\geq 1-e^{-c_{1}t}-e^{-c_{2}t}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}|w_{ij}|\|\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\|\leq t)

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{P}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}|w_{ij}|\|\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\|>t)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}(|w_{ij}|\|\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\|>t)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n\cdot(e^{-c_{1i}t}+e^{-c_{2i}t}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{W}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}\|_{\infty}\leq t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\max_{j\in[d]}\|\sum_{i=1}w_{ij}\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\|\leq t)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\max_{j\in[d]}\sum_{i=1}|w_{ij}|\|\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\|\leq t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\forall j\in[d],\sum_{i=1}|w_{ij}|\|\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\|\leq t)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{P}(\sum_{i=1}|w_{ij}|\|\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\|\leq t)-(d-1)

\displaystyle\geq 1-dn(e^{-c_{1}t}+e^{-c_{2}t}).

By taking
t=(\log(dn))^{2}
, we have

\lambda_{n}=C\cdot\frac{7}{\tau}\left\|\frac{1}{n}(\boldsymbol{W}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W})\right\|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{(\log(dn))^{2}}{n}\right).

∎

Appendix H
FAMAR-Sparse with Estimated
\boldsymbol{F}_{i}

Recall that we defined
\boldsymbol{W}:=(\boldsymbol{w}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{w}_{n})^{\top}
,
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{W}:=(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{w}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{}\boldsymbol{w}_{n})^{\top}
, and
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}:=(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{1},\ldots,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{n})^{\top}
, where
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}:=(\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{i})^{\top},\vect(\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top})^{-1})^{\top})^{\top}
.
The estimators solves the following optimization problem:

\big(\widehat{\boldsymbol{A}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}\big)\in\argmin_{\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{B}}\left\{\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(y_{i}-\left\langle\boldsymbol{A},\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}}_{i}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{B},\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{i}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\lambda_{n}\|\vect(\boldsymbol{B})\|_{1}\right\}.

The analysis on
\widehat{\boldsymbol{A}}
is similar to that of Theorem
12
based on the fact that
\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{U}}=\boldsymbol{0}
, which leads to

\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{A})=\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{y}.

The consistency of
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B}
is established in Theorem
15
. Before proving Theorem
15
, we first introduce a technical lemma and proposition.

Lemma 37
.

Suppose
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times r}
and
\mathbf{B},\mathbf{C}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
and
\left\|\mathbf{CB}^{-1}\right\|<1
, where
\|\cdot\|
is an induced norm. Then
\left\|\mathbf{A}\left[(\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{C})^{-1}-\mathbf{B}^{-1}\right]\right\|\leq\frac{\left\|\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{-1}\right\|\cdot\left\|\mathbf{CB}^{-1}\right\|}{1-\left\|\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}^{-1}\right\|}
.

Proposition 38
.

For the element-wise error of
\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{i}
’s, we have the following results.

(a)

\max_{j\in[p_{1}],k\in[p_{2}]}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|u_{i,jk}-\widehat{u}_{i,jk}|=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)^{1/2}\right).

(b)

\max_{j\in[p_{1}],k\in[p_{2}]}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|u_{i,jk}-\widehat{u}_{i,jk}|^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{log^{2}p_{1}p_{2}}{n^{2}}+\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)\right)
;

(c)

\max_{j\in[p_{1}],k\in[p_{2}]}|u_{i,jk}-\widehat{u}_{i,jk}|=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\left[\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)\right]^{1/2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log np_{1}p_{2}}{p_{2}p_{2}}}\right)
.

Proof of Proposition
38
.

To start with, By Definition
4
(a), we have

\left\|\widehat{\mathbb{F}}-\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}\right\|_{F}^{2}=\frac{1}{p_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}}\|\mathbb{U}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{W}_{1})\|_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{p_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}}\|\mathbb{U}\|_{F}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{W}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{n}{p_{1}p_{2}}\right).

Moreover, similar to
Fan et al. (2013)
, we have

\frac{1}{n}\|\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{U}\|_{\max}=\max_{j\in[p_{1}p_{2}],k\in[k_{1}k_{2}]}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{ij}f_{ik}\right|=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}}\right).

Thus, by Proposition
26
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{p_{1}k_{1}}\boldsymbol{E}_{p_{2}p_{1}}\boldsymbol{S}_{p_{2}p_{1}}\left[\big(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{U}\right]^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}_{k_{2}k_{1}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}_{k_{2}k_{1}}^{\top}\right\|_{\max}+\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{2}}{np_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{np_{1}}\sqrt{p_{2}}\|\boldsymbol{E}_{p_{2}p_{1}}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{S}_{p_{2}p_{1}}\|_{2}\dfrac{1}{\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbb{F}}/n)}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\|_{2}\|\mathbb{F}^{\top}\mathbb{U}\|_{\max}\|\boldsymbol{S}_{k_{2}k_{1}}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{E}_{k_{2}k_{1}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{np_{2}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}\log p_{1}p_{2}}{np_{2}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

Similarly, we have
\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{R}-\mathring{}\boldsymbol{R}\|_{\max}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{2}\log p_{1}p_{2}}{np_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)
.
Therefore, by triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[p_{1}],k\in[p_{2}]}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\widehat{u}_{i,jk}-u_{i,jk}|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|\|\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\|_{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|\|\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\|_{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|\|\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\|_{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)^{1/2}\right).

Similarly, by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[p_{1}],k\in[p_{2}]}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\widehat{u}_{i,jk}-u_{i,jk}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 7\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+7\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+7\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+7\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+7\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+7\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+7\max_{j\in[p_{1}]}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{c}_{s}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}-\mathring{\boldsymbol{R}}_{j\cdot}\right\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}\max_{k\in[p_{2}]}\left\|\mathring{\boldsymbol{C}}_{k\cdot}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{log^{2}p_{1}p_{2}}{n^{2}}+\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)\right).

Furthermore, replacing the average over
i
in the above inequality with maximum over
i
and
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}-\boldsymbol{H}_{1}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}
with
k_{1}k_{2}\|\widehat{\mathbb{F}}-\mathbb{F}(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}\|_{\max}
, we can can also derive

\max_{i\in[n],j\in[p_{1}],k\in[p_{2}]}|\widehat{u}_{i,jk}-u_{i,jk}|^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{log^{2}p_{1}p_{2}}{n^{2}}+\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}+\frac{\log np_{1}p_{2}}{p_{1}p_{2}}\right)\right).

∎

H.1
Proof of Theorem
15

Proof of Theorem
15
.

Replacing the true
(\boldsymbol{F}_{i},\boldsymbol{U}_{i})
in MFM (
1
) by the estimated
(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}.\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{i})
, we have

\displaystyle y_{i}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{F}_{i},\boldsymbol{A}^{*}\right\rangle+\left\langle\boldsymbol{U}_{i},\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\right\rangle+\varepsilon_{i}

\displaystyle=\left\langle(\boldsymbol{H}_{1}^{-1})^{\top}\widehat{}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}^{-1},\boldsymbol{A}^{*}\right\rangle+\left\langle\widehat{}\boldsymbol{U}_{i},\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\right\rangle+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\varepsilon$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\varepsilon$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i},

where
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\varepsilon$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}:=y_{i}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
.
Consider the optimization problem

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\in\argmin_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}p_{2}+K_{1}K_{2}}}\left\{L_{n}(\boldsymbol{y},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\boldsymbol{\theta})+\lambda_{n}\|\vect(\boldsymbol{B})\|_{1}\right\}.

We have
\|\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B})-\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|\leq\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|
for any norm
\|\cdot\|
.

To apply Proposition
35
, we need to show that the conditions on
\boldsymbol{W}
needed to hold for Proposition
35
also hold for

\boldsymbol{W}

.
We start with checking the restricted strong convexity.
By Proposition
7
,
8
, and
38
(c), we have

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n],j\in[d]}|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}-w_{ij}|=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\left[\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)\right]^{1/2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log np_{1}p_{2}}{p_{2}p_{2}}}\right).

Moreover, by Assumption
34
and maximal tail inequality, we have

\max_{j\in[p_{1}],k\in[p_{2}]}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i,jk}-\widehat{w}_{i,jk}|=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)^{1/2}\right).

Also, by Assumption
34
and maximal tail inequality, we have
\max_{i\in[n],j\in[d]}|w_{ik}|=\mathcal{O}_{p}(\sqrt{\log(np_{1}p_{2})})
. Therefore, for
j\in[d]
,

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[d]}\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{iS}-w_{ij}\boldsymbol{w}_{iS}\|_{1}

(57)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\in[d]}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{iS}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}\boldsymbol{w}_{iS}\|_{1}+\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}\boldsymbol{w}_{iS}-w_{ij}\boldsymbol{w}_{iS}\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\in[d]}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}|\sum_{k\in S}|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ik}-w_{ik}|+|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}-w_{ij}|\sum_{k\in S}|w_{ik}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in[n],j\in[d]}|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}|\cdot\sum_{k\in S}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ik}-w_{ik}|\right]+\max_{i\in[n],j\in[d]}|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}-w_{ij}|\cdot\sum_{k\in S}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|w_{ik}|\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left\{\sqrt{\log(np_{1}p_{2})}\cdot|S|\left[\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\left[\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)\right]^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\log np_{1}p_{2}}{p_{2}p_{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1).

Let
\alpha_{\infty}=\|(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}-\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})\|_{\infty}
. With the assumption
\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq\kappa_{\infty}^{-1}
and bound (
57
), we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\|\frac{1}{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})\|_{\infty}

(58)

\displaystyle=\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\max_{j\in S}\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{iS}-w_{ij}\boldsymbol{w}_{iS}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\infty}}\cdot C\sqrt{\log(np_{1}p_{2})}\cdot|S|\left[\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\left[\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)\right]^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\log np_{1}p_{2}}{p_{2}p_{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{2}

with high probability for some constant
C
when
n,p_{1},p_{2}
are large enough.
Hence, by Lemma
37
, we have

\displaystyle\|n(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S})^{-1}-n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\infty}}{1-\alpha_{\infty}}\leq\frac{2\alpha_{\infty}}{\kappa_{\infty}}\leq\frac{1}{2\kappa_{\infty}}.

Together with Assumption
14
and triangle inequality, we have

\|n(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq\|+\frac{1}{2\kappa_{\infty}}\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\infty}},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{|S|}\|\frac{1}{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}\|_{\infty}.

Meanwhile, for matrix 2-norm, let
\alpha_{2}=\|(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}-\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})\|_{2}
. Then

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{2}\|\frac{1}{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{2}\sqrt{|S|}\|\frac{1}{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}}\cdot C\sqrt{\log(p_{1}p_{2})}|S|^{3/2}\left[\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\left[\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)\right]^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\log np_{1}p_{2}}{p_{2}p_{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{2}

By Lemma
37
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|n(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S})^{-1}-n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\right\|_{2}\leq\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{2}}{1-\alpha_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2\kappa_{2}}.

when
p_{1},p_{2}
are large enough.
Together with Assumption
14
and triangle inequality, we have

\|n(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S})^{-1}\|_{2}\leq\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{2\kappa_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}}.

Therefore, the restricted strong convexity holds for

\boldsymbol{W}

.

We also need to show that the irrepresentable condition holds for

\boldsymbol{W}

.

\displaystyle\left\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S_{2}}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S})^{-1}-\boldsymbol{W}_{S_{2}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\frac{1}{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S_{2}}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}_{S_{2}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}\big\|_{\infty}\|n(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}+\left\|\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}_{S_{2}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}\left[n(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S})^{-1}-n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\right]\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle I+II.

By (
57
) and Assumption
14
(a), we have

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\max_{j\in S_{2}}\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{iS}-w_{ij}\boldsymbol{w}_{iS}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\infty}}\cdot C\sqrt{\log(np_{1}p_{2})}\cdot|S|\left[\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\left[\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)\right]^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\log np_{1}p_{2}}{p_{2}p_{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right].

with high probability.
By Lemma
37
and Assumption
14
(b), we have

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{W}_{S_{2}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\frac{\|(\frac{1}{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})\cdot n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}}{1-\|(\frac{1}{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})\cdot n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|(\frac{1}{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})\|_{\infty}\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}}{1-\|(\frac{1}{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}-\frac{1}{n}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})\|_{\infty}\|n(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\frac{1}{\kappa_{\infty}}\cdot C\sqrt{\log(np_{1}p_{2})}\cdot|S|\left[\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\left[\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)\right]^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\log np_{1}p_{2}}{p_{2}p_{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right]}{1-1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C}{\kappa_{\infty}}\cdot C\sqrt{\log(np_{1}p_{2})}\cdot|S|\left[\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\left[\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)\right]^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\log np_{1}p_{2}}{p_{2}p_{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right]

with high probability, where the third inequality is by (
57
).

Combining these two bounds, we have

\displaystyle\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S_{2}}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S})^{-1}-\boldsymbol{W}_{S_{2}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S}(\boldsymbol{W}_{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sqrt{\log(p_{1}p_{2})}|S|\left[\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\left[\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)\right]^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\log np_{1}p_{2}}{p_{2}p_{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right]\leq\tau/2

with high probability when
n,p_{1},p_{2}
are large enough.
Therefore, by triangle inequality and Assumption
14
(b), we have

\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S_{2}}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1-\tau+\frac{\tau}{2}=1-\frac{\tau}{2}.

To sum up, we have verified that the conditions needed for Proposition
35
also holds for

\boldsymbol{W}

, and thus, by Proposition
35
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{6}{5\kappa_{\infty}}\left(\left\|\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{\infty}+\lambda\right),

(59)

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{4}{\kappa_{2}}\left(\left\|\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}+\lambda\sqrt{\left|S_{1}\right|}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\leq\min\left\{\frac{6}{5\kappa_{\infty}}\left(\left\|\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{1}+\lambda\left|S_{1}\right|\right),\frac{4\sqrt{|S|}}{\kappa_{2}}\left(\left\|\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}+\lambda\sqrt{\left|S_{1}\right|}\right)\right\}.

Notice that

\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)=\frac{1}{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{S}^{\top}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{y}).

Thus

\left\|\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\delta$\kern 0.0pt}}},\quad\left\|\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\delta$\kern 0.0pt}}}\sqrt{|S|},\quad\left\|\nabla_{S}L_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{y},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{W}$\kern 0.0pt}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right)\right\|_{1}\leq\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\delta$\kern 0.0pt}}}|S|.

In addition,
\lambda>14\delta/\tau\geq\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\delta$\kern 0.0pt}}}
. We conclude the proof by substituting the above bounds to (
59
).

∎

H.2
Proof of Theorem
16

Proof of Theorem
16
.

Define
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{*}=(\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top},[(\boldsymbol{H}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{H}_{1})^{\top}(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{C}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}})\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})]^{\top})^{\top}
. Then we still have
\|\vect(\widehat{}\boldsymbol{B})-\vect(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|\leq\|\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{*}\|
for any norm
\|\cdot\|
. Also,
\boldsymbol{w}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}^{\top}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{*}
.
Let

\delta=\max_{j\in S}\delta_{j},

where

\displaystyle\delta_{j}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{w}$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{i}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\boldsymbol{\theta}$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{*}-y_{i})\right|=\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-y_{i})\right|=\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}\varepsilon_{i}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{ij}\varepsilon_{i}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}-w_{ij})\varepsilon_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{ij}\varepsilon_{i}\right|+\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 2.15277pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$w$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}-w_{ij})^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{logp_{1}p_{2}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)^{1/2}\right),

where the inequality is by triangle inequality, Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, and Proposition
38
(b).
By union bound, we have

\delta=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{|S|\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}+|S|\sqrt{\frac{\log p_{1}p_{2}}{n}}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p_{2}}\vee\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\right)^{1/2}\right).

∎

Appendix I
Extra Experiment Results

I.1
FAMAR Coefficient Estimation and Prediction

As an extension of the simulations in Section
4
, we present three more settings with a different way of generating true
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}
. Recall that previously in Section
4
,
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}
is generated as
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}=0.5\cdot\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{B}*\boldsymbol{C}
. As mentioned in Section
1
, we can also deal with cases where
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}
is not related with
\boldsymbol{R}^{\top}\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\boldsymbol{C}
at all. To show this in simulations, Figure
9
to
10
present the result for matrix factor and idiosyncratic component estimation, FAMAR coefficient estimation, and dependent variable prediction. The detailed settings for each group of simulations are as follows.

•

Figure
8
: Fix
n=1000
, increase
p_{1}=p_{2}
from
20
to
100
. All other settings are the same as that for simulations in Figure
4
except that
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}
is generated such that each elements are i.i.d from
{\cal N}(1000,1000^{2})
.

•

Figure
9
: All other settings are the same as that for simulations in Figure
8
except that the elements of
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}
are i.i.d. from
{\cal N}(p_{1}p_{2},(p_{1}p_{2})^{2})
, with mean and standard deviation growing with the dimension
p_{1},p_{2}
.

•

Figure
10
: Fix
(p_{1},p_{2})=(70,50)
, increase
n
from
500
to
4000
. All other settings are the same as that for simulations in Figure
5
except that
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}
is generated such that each elements are i.i.d from
{\cal N}(1000,1000^{2})
.

The results are mostly similar to that in Section
4
, showing the efficiency of FAMAR.
Notice that the trend of relative errors on
y
is different from what we see in Figure
4
as
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
increases. Since the dimension of factors is fixed as (2,2) and does not change with
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
, the contribution of
\left\langle\boldsymbol{A}^{*},\boldsymbol{F}_{i}\right\rangle
decreases as the dimension increases. To verify that, we make the elements in
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}
to have mean and std grow with problem dimensions with order
O(\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}})
. The results are shown in Figure
9
, where the errors on
y
present similar patterns as that in Figure
4
.

Figure 8
:
Fix
n=1000
, increase
p_{1}=p_{2}
from
20
to
100
.
\boldsymbol{A}^{*}
is generated such that each elements are i.i.d from
{\cal N}(1000,1000^{2})
. The blue, orange, and green lines correspond to doing the regression with true
(\boldsymbol{F},\boldsymbol{U})
, estimated
(\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}})
, and
\boldsymbol{X}
, respectively. The experiments are repeated 100 times. The solid lines are the means, the shadows show the standard deviation, and the vertical lines present the quartiles.

, increase

.

. The blue, orange, and green lines correspond to doing the regression with true
(\boldsymbol{F},\boldsymbol{U})
, estimated
(\widehat{\boldsymbol{F}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{U}})
, and
\boldsymbol{X}
, respectively. The experiments are repeated 100 times. The solid lines are the means, the shadows show the standard deviation, and the vertical lines present the quartiles.
